# Backlog — done (archive)

Rows that have landed, newest first. Split out of [BACKLOG.md](BACKLOG.md) by
**A8**, 2026-08-12, when that file reached 76 KB and every run on three machines
was reading all of it to find the handful of rows it could act on.

**Rows here are verbatim** — archiving never rewrites one. A row arrives here
only when its work has *landed*: `IN-REVIEW` means the PRs are open, `DONE`
means they merged. Flipping a row and moving it is a STEP 4 chore, described in
[BACKLOG.md](BACKLOG.md).

This file is history, not queue. An agent picking work reads
[BACKLOG.md](BACKLOG.md); come here for precedent — how a past stage was
verified, what a previous run measured, why a decision went the way it did.

## Done

On this run that call returned **HTTP 503** (`"No server is currently available to service your request"`) five times over ~50s, and by direct `curl` as well. **The credential was fine and the fleet was not at risk:** the same token got **200** from `/rate_limit` and from the private `/repos/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth`, `x-oauth-scopes: repo` with no `workflow` as intended, and GraphQL `{ viewer { login } }` answered **`tide-rack-bot`, databaseId 314850083** — matching the `GIT_*` noreply address the prompt hard-codes. [githubstatus](https://www.githubstatus.com) read *Partially Degraded Service* with API Requests, Issues, Pull Requests and Actions all degraded; only the REST `/user` shard was down. **The gap is that the prompt conflates two different failures.** *Wrong login* is the dangerous one the rule was written for — a silent fallback to Jeff's bypass-listed credential. *Cannot determine login* is a different thing, and a hard STOP on it means a routine GitHub wobble silently costs a run, on a box that runs daily. **Scope** STEP 0.7 in `docs/weekly-run-prompt.md`: accept GraphQL `viewer { login }` as an equivalent second path (it is a genuine assertion, not a weakening — it reads the same token and returns the same identity), and separate *asserted-wrong* (STOP, always) from *could-not-assert* (retry both paths, then STOP and journal). **Accept** a run whose REST `/user` is 503 but whose GraphQL viewer is `tide-rack-bot` proceeds, and says in its journal which path it used; a run that gets any *wrong* login still stops dead. **Size** minutes — wording only. **Precedent** this run did exactly that and recorded the substitution rather than assuming. |

| A26 | 2026-08-19 | any | **IN-REVIEW 2026-08-19 (linux)** — TideSynth PR below. **The fix is NOT the one this row proposed, and the difference is the finding.** Narrowing the range (via `--range` or the upstream) would hide a foreign commit that an earlier attempt of the same run had already pushed — which is exactly why the old docstring rejected the upstream, and it was right. The range was never the problem; the **verdict** was. Severity is now per commit: **misattributed and NOT yet pushed → BLOCKING, exit 1** (A14's case, `--amend --reset-author` available, unchanged); **misattributed and ALREADY pushed → ADVISORY, exit 0, printed in full** (the run cannot rewrite it, so failing demands the one action STEP 4 forbids). Nothing hidden either way; `--strict` restores the old behaviour. **A/B on a real branch, not just synthetic:** `tide/win/competitive-review` carries three genuine Jeff-authored interactive commits — old `rc=1` "do not push", new `rc=0` with all three disclosed. **Adds `--selftest`, five cases, and it earned itself immediately:** the first implementation compared `%h` against `git rev-list`'s full SHAs, so nothing ever matched the unpushed set and EVERY commit read as "already pushed" — a silent, total loss of A14's protection in a check whose whole job is not to be silently wrong. Real-repo spot checks all still passed, because none had a misattributed commit to misclassify. `FORMAT` now uses `%H`. STEP 4's wording in `docs/weekly-run-prompt.md` updated to match — **it changes a rule, so it wants Jeff's eye**, as this row said. **DOING 2026-08-19 (linux)** — branch `tide/linux/A26-authorship-range`, claimed before work per STEP 2. **STEP 2's continue-a-branch rule and STEP 4's authorship check contradict each other, and the check loses. Found 2026-08-19 (macos, scheduled run) by tripping it.** STEP 2 says an open PR naming your item from your own platform means "resume on it — do not start a fresh branch". When the branch was started by an INTERACTIVE session, its commits are authored `Jeff McClintock` by that session's own convention. `check-commit-authorship.py` defaults to `origin/<default>..HEAD`, so it sees those commits, reports `2 NOT authored by tide-rack-bot` and says **"Do not push"** — for commits that were already pushed before the run began and that STEP 4 separately forbids rewriting ("never rewrite anything already pushed"). **The run is left with a check it cannot satisfy and cannot act on**, which is the same shape as the STEP 1 / STEP 5 standoff that sat unresolved through three platform breaks (**A17**, **A19**). **The workaround exists and should be written down rather than rediscovered:** pass `--range <branch-tip-as-you-found-it>..HEAD` to check only the commits this run actually made. Verified both ways this run — default range flags Jeff's `e01bb72`/`6f49bba`, `--range e01bb72..HEAD` reports "all commits authored by tide-rack-bot" for the run's own two. **The danger of leaving it is precisely that the check cries wolf**: a run that learns to push past "Do not push" on a continued branch is a run that will push past it on the day it means what A14 wrote it to mean. **Scope** `scripts/check-commit-authorship.py` plus the STEP 4 wording in `docs/weekly-run-prompt.md`. **Accept** a run continuing an interactively-started branch gets a clean check without passing anything by hand — e.g. the script defaults to the upstream tracking ref's pre-run tip, or STEP 4 states the `--range` form as the rule for continued branches. **Size** small, but it changes a rule, so the prompt edit wants Jeff's eye. **Note** `.github/workflows/**` is NOT involved, so the bot can do this one. |

E1 4/4, all five host fixtures as expected. **Row as it stood at IN-REVIEW follows, kept for the record:** **GATE AND PITCH BOTH WORK, MEASURED 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive, Jeff directing) — Jeff's root-MIDI-CV design does what it was meant to.** `tests/hosts/v3-midi-pitch.rpp`: **0.05–0.45 s silent · 0.60–1.10 s 311.0 Hz · 1.35–1.95 s silent**, and the pitch now TRACKS the note instead of sitting at the Oscillator's own default. **Tracking is exact:** a two-note fixture an octave apart renders **311.2 Hz and 622.2 Hz — ratio 1.9994**, i.e. 12 MIDI semitones produce 11.99 semitones of output, so 1 V/octave is right to within 0.05%. **THE DESIGN.** Every fresh document now gets, at the ROOT: `MIDI In` → `SE MIDI to CV 2` → the **TIDE MIDI-CV** facade (`TideApp::seedRootMidiCv`). The facade is a prefab holding nothing but jacks, each fed inward from the root MIDI-CV through the container's own pins — so the polyphonic source stays at the root where polyphony is legal, and the container only has to carry CV inward, which is an ordinary connection. That side-steps **E7** rather than needing it fixed. One MIDI-CV per project, TIDE-owned, so "what if the user adds a second" never arises. **HOW THE FACADE IS FOUND.** `AddModule` returns −1 for a prefab because a prefab may hold several top-level modules, so the code snapshots the root's module handles and diffs — the same idiom `EditorScreenshot/EditorCommandDispatcher.cpp:1399` already uses, with `dynamic_cast<CUG*>` as the necessary filter because a container's child list holds `CLine2` connections alongside modules. **PIN CONTRACT, verified for 1, 2 and 4 jacks:** the facade's outer input pin is `7 + jack index` (a Container's 0..6 are its own built-ins, pin 2 being Visible), so 7=PITCH 8=GATE 9=VEL 10=TRIG. `build-prefabs.py` and `TideApp.cpp` both say so; change one and the other changes. **A TRAP PAID FOR:** coordinates are DOCUMENT space and the canvas is centred near **4000** — a user-dropped prefab lands around X 4024–4288, Y 3944–4008. Seeding at `{40,40}` "works" and puts everything in the far top-left, off the visible rack, looking exactly like the insert having failed. **WHAT IS LEFT is a tuning constant, not a design problem:** note 60 sounds 311.0 Hz where 261.6 Hz is wanted — a fixed **+3 semitone** offset, i.e. 0.25 V between `Oscillator`'s V→Hz reference and `SE MIDI to CV 2`'s note→V. Builds: `TIDE_VST3` **Debug and Release**, plus the shared-code consumers `SynthEditCL`, `SynthEdit_VST3`, `SynthEdit_GMPI` — all SUCCEEDED, so this is not "TIDE builds now". `dsp_tests` is **unchanged at 13 passed / 44 failed, identical with and without the change** (A/B'd by stashing it) — those failures are pre-existing and unrelated, filed as **S16**. **NOT verified, and the row's Accept therefore is not met by me:** a Debug `TIDE_VST3` loading a project in REAPER. Computer-use is refused during a scheduled run and there is no runnable standalone TIDE, so nobody has watched the Debug build survive a load. **Left IN-REVIEW for that reason — an interactive session should do that one check.** **Original row follows.** **TIDE cannot run as a Debug build — found 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive) while verifying S11.** A Debug `TIDE_VST3` aborts REAPER during load: `Assertion failed: (false), function RegisterExternalPluginsXmlOnce, file UgDatabase.cpp, line 549`. **Cause:** that function reads `database.se.xml` from the bundle, `doc.Error()` is true because **the resource is not staged into the bundle in either config** (`Contents/Resources/` holds only `ControlsXp.xml`), and the error path is `assert(false); return;`. In Release the assert compiles out and it silently returns — which is why every previous session worked and nobody noticed. **Why it matters more than it looks:** it means the one build with asserts and symbols cannot be used to debug the crashes this project keeps chasing, so every investigation has had to work from `.ips` reports and stderr. | A16 | 2026-08-18 | any | **Landed 2026-08-18 — [#119](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/119) merged 2026-08-17T18:29:33Z; flipped and archived by the macos run that filed it.** **REPRODUCED AND GUARDED 2026-08-17 (macos, scheduled) — [#119](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/119).** **The repro the original filing could not get:** the signature is matched exactly by a concurrent **PARTIAL** index reset landing between the add and the commit — `git add f1..f5`, then `git reset -q HEAD -- f2 f3 f4 f5` from the other session, then `git commit` **exits 0 and commits f1 alone, with all five still correct in the working tree.** That is the filed symptom to the letter (5 staged, 1 landed, tree intact). **Two neighbouring shapes were tested and are NOT this bug, which is what makes the diagnosis specific rather than plausible:** a *full* `git reset` makes the commit fail loudly (`nothing added to commit`), and a peer running `git commit` first is the **A14** shape (wrong author, right content). **Only the partial unstage both succeeds and lies.** `git checkout HEAD -- <path>` did not reproduce it either. **So A16's "most likely cause ... plausible but not proven" is now proven**, and the mechanism is narrower than "a race on `.git/index`": it is specifically an operation that unstages a *subset*. **Guard:** `scripts/check-commit-completeness.py`, in the shape of `check-commit-authorship.py`. **Necessarily two-phase** — `--record` before the commit, `--verify` after — because the race destroys the very list a post-hoc check would need, so there is no one-shot version. The manifest lives in `.git/`, so it can never itself be committed; `--verify` with no manifest is a **skip, not a failure**, so adding it cannot break anything that predates it. **Accept met:** `--selftest` builds throwaway repos and proves all three cases — clean commit passes, partial unstage fails (4 missing correctly named), full unstage fails. It was also run for real on this row's own commits. **Prompt:** STEP 3 gains the record/verify pair, STEP 4 gains a line stating that authorship and completeness are blind to each other — the 2026-08-15 short commit was correctly authored, and the 2026-08-15 foreign commit had the right content. **NOT done, and deliberately:** wiring it into `lint.yml` as a CI step, because that is `.github/workflows/**` and the bot token structurally cannot push it. CI cannot see a pre-commit manifest anyway, so this guard is inherently local; a CI version would have to check something else. **Original row follows.** **A `git add`+`git commit` in a shared working copy can silently commit fewer files than were staged.** Filed 2026-08-15, same session as A14, different failure shape: mid-C11, `git add <5 paths>` followed immediately by `git commit` produced a commit containing only 1 of the 5 files. The working tree held all five, correctly, throughout — nothing was lost or overwritten, and A14's authorship check would have passed cleanly, since the short commit was correctly authored as `tide-rack-bot`. **A14 does not catch this**: it asserts *who* wrote a commit, not that the commit contains *what was staged*. The most likely cause is a race on `.git/index` from a concurrent git operation in the other session sharing this checkout — plausible but not proven; no reliable repro was attempted, since recovering the missing content and moving on was the higher priority in the moment. **Cheapest workable guard:** immediately after any commit in a shared checkout, `git show --stat HEAD` and eyeball it against the file list just staged, before doing anything else — this is what caught it this session, entirely by habit, not by an automated check. A script version would compare `git diff --cached --name-only` captured before the commit against `git show --stat HEAD`'s file list after it, and fail loudly on a mismatch. **Scope** a `scripts/check-commit-completeness.py` in the shape of `check-commit-authorship.py`, plus a STEP 3/4 line in the run prompt. **Accept** a deliberately short commit (stage N files, commit N-1 of them via a race simulated with a background `git reset` mid-commit, if that's even reproducible) is detected before push. **Size** unknown until the repro is attempted — this is the part A14 didn't need, since authorship races are trivial to simulate and index-write races may not be. |

re-pushing on a timer is the hack to avoid. **RETRACTION:** the previous entry's "MIDI delivery stops across a document rebuild" is **wrong** — MIDI never stopped; the **transport** had stopped, because clicking in the editor to place modules halts playback. use the byte-safe `b'\r\n'`-aware edit helper. **S12(a) CONTRACT WORKED OUT 2026-08-17 (Jeff ruled: synthesise container IO, least disruptive to SynthEdit). Read from the IMPORTER, so it is exact:** **(1) container IO plug** — any `<Plug>` with a `Direction` attribute is one (that is literally how `ug_base::Setup` identifies it): `<Plug Direction="0" Datatype="2"/>` = MIDI **in**, since `EPlugDataType{DT_ENUM=0, DT_TEXT=1, DT_MIDI2=2, DT_DOUBLE=3, DT_BOOL=4, DT_FSAMPLE=5}`. **(2) IO Mod tie** (only honoured when the module has `UGF_IO_MOD`): `<Plug Direction="1" Datatype="2" TiedTo="<containerHandle>" TiedToPinIdx="<n>"/>` → `up->TiedTo = p2; p2->TiedTo = up;`. **(3) connections:** `<Line From="<handle>" To="<handle>" FromPin="i" ToPin="j"/>`, pins default 0, handles via `HandleToObject`. **CONSTRAINT THAT SHAPES THE FIX — the browser's "MIDI In" module CANNOT be the endpoint in a plug-in.** `modules_internal/MidiIn.h` is `class MidiIn : public MpBase2, public ISpecialIoModule`; it self-registers via `AudioMaster()->RegisterIoModule(this)` in `open()`, which in the standalone reaches `UIoManager` (fed from a MIDI device) but in the plug-in reaches **`SynthRuntime::RegisterIoModule(ISpecialIoModule*) { return 1; } // nothing special to do in plugin`** — so it registers and is never fed. Its Audio pins confirm it is a source only (`MIDI Data` out, `Activity` out, `MPE Mode` in — **no MIDI input pin**, so nothing can be routed into it either). **The classic plug-in path is therefore the only one available and is exactly the ruling:** host → `vst_in` → **synth container's DT_MIDI2 plug** → **IO Mod** inside → the user's MIDI-CV 2 etc. **REMAINING CHOICE IS UX, AND IT IS JEFF'S: (i)** synthesise container plug + tied IO Mod at export and let users patch from the IO Mod (already in TIDE's module list; **no SynthEdit change**), or **(ii)** keep "MIDI In" as the user-facing source and teach `SynthRuntime` to feed registered MIDI modules like `UIoManager` does (nicer UX, but it is the SynthEdit change the ruling was avoiding). **S12(a) ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-08-17 — suspect (a) REFUTED, and the real break is one layer lower: TIDE's rack container exposes NO MIDI plug and NO audio plugs.** **(a) is wrong:** `MpeConverter::processMidi` opens with `if (isMidi2Message(msg)) { sink(msg, timestamp); return; }` — **MIDI 2.0 passes through by design**, so the UMP packets TIDE forwards are exactly what SE accepts; nothing needs translating. **The chain probe (four points, one run) shows the break:** `SetupVstIO: synthModule=1 plugs=3` / `synth plug: type=0`, `type=4`, `type=0` / `AudioMaster::MidiIn len=8 [40 90 3c] audioIn=1` / `vst_in sink: size=8 **inUse=0** mo=1`. With `EPlugDataType = DT_ENUM 0, DT_TEXT 1, DT_MIDI2 2, DT_DOUBLE 3, DT_BOOL 4, DT_FSAMPLE 5`, those three plugs are **ENUM/BOOL/ENUM** — no DT_MIDI2, no DT_FSAMPLE. `SetupVstIO` scans exactly those for MIDI and audio, finds neither, connects nothing, and the MIDI is sent into an unconnected plug. **This also explains the asymmetry:** audio works NOT via container plugs but because **Sound Out is a special IO module** (`ug_soundcard_out` calls `RegisterIoModule` at `Open()` and takes the host's buffers directly); MIDI has no such registration, so it depends on plumbing that does not exist. **Fix direction:** give the inner rack container real plugin IO — a `DT_MIDI2` input plug wired to the patch's MIDI In module (plus `DT_FSAMPLE` outputs if audio should ever leave via the container rather than Sound Out's registration); in SE terms that is an **IO Mod**, and `exportDspXml`'s synthetic outer container is the natural place to synthesise it. **Design question for Jeff: should TIDE instead make the patch's "MIDI In" module a registering special IO module — the symmetric counterpart of Sound Out?** **Build trap recorded:** the first probe run logged nothing because `cmake --fresh` had cleared `SYNTHEDITLIB_FOLDER_OVERRIDE`, so the build compiled SynthEditLib fetched from GitHub and local edits were invisible — `strings` the installed binary for the probe's log path before believing a silent probe. **S12(a) MEASURED 2026-08-17 — MIDI reaches the processor but NOT the graph.** (1) **Proven arriving:** a temp probe in `onMidiMessage` logged 42 messages with `prepared=1` during a looping C4 — note-on `0x90`, note-off `0x80`, note `0x3c` — all forwarded to `rack.MidiIn`; **they are 8-byte MIDI 2.0 UMP packets** (leading `0x40`), not MIDI 1.0 bytes. (3) **A COMPLETE instrument is silent:** per Jeff, an osc wired straight to Sound Out drones at default pitch regardless of MIDI and proves nothing — so the patch was rebuilt properly (MIDI In → MIDI-CV 2; Pitch → Phase Dist Osc; **Gate → VCA Volume**; Osc → VCA Signal; VCA → Sound Out) and the measured peak is **−156.7 dB = digital silence** through the note. **Two suspects, in order: (a) MIDI FORMAT** — GMPI delivers UMP while `SeAudioMaster::MidiIn` may want MIDI 1.0; `se_vst3`'s `SeProcessor` does explicit translation (`midi2data`/`midi2size`, advertises `kMIDIProtocol_2_0`) that TIDE's one-line forward does not — compare those call sites first; **(b) CONTAINER PLUMBING** — `SetupVstIO` connects VST Input's "MIDI Out" to *the synth container's* MIDI plug, and S12 wraps the flat rack in a **synthetic outer container**, so MIDI may land on the outer container and never reach the inner rack holding the MIDI In module. **Method note:** a tight Lua `Track_GetPeakInfo` loop measures NOTHING — it blocks REAPER's main thread and re-reads one frozen snapshot (9.3M "samples", transport position never advancing); use `reaper.defer`, one sample per main-thread cycle. **Also:** TIDE's set has **no MIDI Monitor** (Diagnostic = 1 kHz Tone + DAW Sample Rate only). **FIRST SOUND 2026-08-17 — the thin-slice Accept is met and HEARD (Jeff: "i can hear it!"): place 1 kHz Tone, place Sound Out, drag the cable — track meter −6.0 dB, master green.** The loop is live end to end: edit → export → chunk param → processor → rebuild → audio. Two findings from one missing habit; add both to how a host check is done. |

| D3 | 2026-08-17 | mac | **DONE 2026-08-17 — IN-REVIEW: [gmpi_ui#9](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/gmpi_ui/pull/9) + [SynthEdit#35](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/SynthEdit/pull/35), which **must merge together** (the CMake one alone changes nothing; the helper one alone leaves iOS linking AppKit). **The fix is not where this row put it.** Making the `if(APPLE)` block iOS-excluding would only turn a COMPILE error into a LINK error: the headers dispatch on `__APPLE__`, true on iOS, so the call sites still reference `browse_to_impl`/`open_url_impl`. **The split belongs in the `.mm` files**, which now choose their framework internally on `TARGET_OS_OSX` — `open_url` uses `NSWorkspace` on macOS and `UIApplication openURL:options:completionHandler:` on iOS; `browse_to` is macOS-only behaviour and a deliberate no-op elsewhere. The CMake change is then just the framework line: AppKit is macOS-only, iOS wants UIKit (CoreText/CoreFoundation/UniformTypeIdentifiers exist on both). **Verified:** TIDE_VST3, SynthEdit_VST3 and SynthEditCL all build on macOS. **Not verified on iOS** — no iOS target exists (**S10**), so this removes the known compile blocker rather than proving an iOS build succeeds. The answer was "do not widen the RMS gate".** The first cross-platform render in the harness's life: macOS against the Linux goldens, three independent engines (local Release V1.6.175, local Debug V1.6.182, published V1.6.183 — all agreeing, so engine version and build config are ruled out). `voice_midi_note`, whose residual is pure rounding, came back at **RMS −123.1 / peak −90.3 dBFS** — exactly 1 LSB on 0.053% of samples against the ~10.7% the −100 dBFS gate allows, a **200× margin**. Finding (e)'s premise (1 LSB on *every* sample) is simply not what cross-platform drift looks like, so the gates stayed at −100/−86. **What did need fixing was a different class of residual nobody had modelled:** `osc_naive_sine` failed at **RMS −73.5 / peak −68.7 dBFS**, and the residual **grows linearly with render duration** (−96 dBFS in the first 0.1 s block, −69 dBFS in the last, zero best-fit lag) — a 0.15 ppm frequency offset, ≈2.5 ULP at single precision, integrating into phase error. **No fixed dBFS number describes a free-running oscillator**, so the fix is a per-case tolerance override with a written reason that the harness prints and reports, not a wider global gate — widening globally would have to reach −67 dBFS and sail past finding (b)'s reference defect at −80.8 dBFS. Five negative controls plus a new `--selftest`; reasoning and numbers in findings (f) and (g) of [docs/e1-verification-harness.md](docs/e1-verification-harness.md). **Original item follows.** **Not work any linux run can do — and it was the linux NEXT pointer for two days. Corrected in prose 2026-08-13.** *(Left as `any` deliberately: the Platform vocabulary is `any`/`win`/`mac`/`linux` and **has no way to say "any box except linux"**, which is exactly what this row is. Tried `mac/win` and A3's `check-backlog-diff.py` correctly rejected it — only the Status cell may change on an existing row, and that value is outside the documented set anyway. So the constraint lives here in the Item text, where a reader will actually hit it. A one-cell change is Jeff's to make if he wants it.)* The Accept clause below is "one render of both cases **on a second platform**" — second *to linux*, which is the only lane that has ever run. So the linux box is the one machine that is definitionally unable to satisfy it, and the NEXT block's old reasoning ("this is the box that can actually test cross-platform") was backwards. Confirmed on the linux box before falling through to P7c: no wine, no container runtime, no macOS emulation — and **Wine would have been worse than nothing anyway**, since its `msvcrt` routes `sin`/`cos`/`exp` to glibc's libm, i.e. to the exact implementation the linux golden was rendered with, so a Wine render would report near-zero drift and license a tolerance that a real Windows box then fails. **Whichever of mac or win picks this up: the render half is the cheap half** (download the engine, run `tools/render_harness.py`, record two numbers); the judgement half is setting the RMS gate from that data. ## Resolved — questions that were blocked on Jeff

Moved verbatim from [BACKLOG.md](BACKLOG.md) by A8, 2026-08-12. Every row in
that section was `RESOLVED`, so a section headed "agents must not start these"
held nothing an agent could be stopped from starting. The rulings themselves are
indexed in [docs/decisions.md](docs/decisions.md); these rows carry the working
detail behind them.

| ID | Status | Item |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | RESOLVED | **Licence chosen 2026-08-07 by Jeff: ISC**, the same licence as the GMPI and gmpi_ui repos. LICENSE files are committed and pushed in both `TideSynth` (`a58a6f1`) and `SynthEditLib` (`a2143a4` — note `42ce33d` briefly added MIT from a misheard decision; ISC replaced it minutes later, both remain in history). This also completes **C1** and unblocks the "open source" claim, which the holding page now makes. C2–C7 were blocked on C0 alone, and C0 was approved 2026-08-08. |
| C0 | RESOLVED | **Carve-out APPROVED 2026-08-08 by Jeff**, with one standing direction: **keep as much `ExportAsPlugin` code private as practical.** C2–C7 are now TODO rather than BLOCKED, and so are S1b's remaining stages and S8. Two consequences of the direction, both recorded in [docs/carve-out.md](docs/carve-out.md): **(1)** `SynthEditCL` **stays private** — `SynthEditCL/main.cpp:1230` calls `ExportAsPlugin` directly, so a public CLI means publishing the export code or splitting the tool, and TIDE embeds patches rather than exporting them so it needs neither. That closes the second open question. **(2)** The plan as written would have **published the export implementation** — `ExportAsPlugin.{cpp,h}` are on `EditorLib/CMakeLists.txt:113-114` unconditionally, and the plan moves everything on that list, so following it literally leaks the one thing the carve-out exists to hold back. Filed as **C1b**, which must run before C2. Verified harmless to TIDE first: `x TIDE_VST3!*ExportAsPlugin*` returns nothing against the Release build while a control query returns hits, so the linker never pulls the object — the boundary already holds at link time, by accident rather than design. **Repo naming answered the same day: keep `SynthEditLib`** — no rename when the editor moves in. **That closes every open question on the carve-out plan:** C0 approved, licence ISC, `SynthEditCL` private, name unchanged. Nothing on it waits on a decision now, only on the work. |
| G1 | RESOLVED | **Repo created, and made PUBLIC 2026-08-07 by Jeff** — <https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth>. Unblocks the "Source" section of the holding page and makes GitHub Pages available free (see H1). **It also makes L1 urgent rather than theoretical:** TideSynth is now in exactly the state PLAN.md criticises `SynthEditLib` for — public, readable by anyone, with no LICENSE file, therefore all-rights-reserved and legally unusable by anyone. Two repos in that state now, not one. |
| H1 | RESOLVED | **tidesynth.com went live 2026-08-08.** `https://tidesynth.com` serves the holding page over a Let's Encrypt cert (SANs cover apex + `www`, auto-renewing), `www` 301s to the apex, and HTTP 301s to HTTPS on all four GitHub edge IPs. Done in an interactive session with Jeff: Pages enabled (Actions source), workflow run, DNS repointed at the registrar, custom domain set, Enforce HTTPS ticked. **The old site was already dead** — the apex pointed at `redirector.servers.netregistry.net`, which served a frameset embedding an Azure static-website endpoint that no longer resolves. **Live email on the domain was preserved** (MX + smtp/imap/pop/pop3/webmail on `nsserver.net.nz`, verified intact on all three nameservers afterwards). Two checklist errors corrected in [docs/hosting.md](docs/hosting.md): the Domainz zone editor is at `/~/dns` and is unreachable from the domain's product page, and the portal's "Export Zone" button 500s — so the zone backup was transcribed by hand into [docs/dns-zone-tidesynth.com.txt](docs/dns-zone-tidesynth.com.txt). `website/CNAME` landed in PR #21 and survived a redeploy. See the JOURNAL entry for 2026-08-08. |

Original finding below. |
